#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "cpu.h"
#include "device.h"
#include "emul.h"
#include "interrupt.h"
#include "machine.h"
#include "memory.h"
#include "misc.h"

#include "thirdparty/mvme187.h"
#include "thirdparty/mvme_pcctworeg.h"


#define	INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK	0x07

#define	PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ	100.0
#define DEV_PCC2_TICK_SHIFT	14


// #define	debug fatal

/*  pcc2widthkills: TWELVE width-guard blocks in DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2) -- TEN of them
    `fatal(); exit(1)' kills that ended the host on a guest-chosen access width, and
    two (IPL, MASK) #119 warn-once latches that declined it process-globally -- an ordinary aligned halfword load of a documented
    register.  Same class as #337 (dev_wdc.c) and #497 (dev_sgi_re.c).

    THE TREE ALREADY KNEW THE WIDTH IS LEGAL: mvme_pcctworeg.h says the PCCchip2
    registers "can be accessed as bytes (8 bits), two-bytes (16 bits), or four-bytes
    (32 bits)".  So this is not a fault to model; it is an access the model declines
    to IMPLEMENT on one side and already implements on the other:

      READS   are served BEFORE the switch by memcpy(data, pcctwo_reg + addr, len),
              raw bytes in address order -- width-correct by construction.  The old
              guard discarded a correct answer.  Now: ACCEPT (fall through).
      WRITES  every arm uses `idata', the composed value, which is "the addressed
              byte" ONLY at len == 1: memory_readmax64 composes big-endian, so
              (uint8_t) idata is the HIGHEST-address lane.  Deleting the guard would
              store the neighbour's byte -- st.h 0xAABB to GENCTL would set GENCTL to
              0xBB, VECBASE's lane.  And eight arms mask, read-modify-write or clear
              on write, and seven of the twelve blocks call reassert_interrupts()
              (five of those eight; a seat measured the earlier "six" wrong).  Now: DECLINE +
              latched announce.  Per-lane composition through each arm (COMPOSE) is
              the faithful upgrade and is FILED as pcc2widecompose; not taken here
              because the hardware's lane semantics are not on disk and no driver
              MEASUREMENT exists either way: the only consumer is mvme187, no mvme187
              boot runs in this harness, and the one OS source in the tree (OpenBSD
              2.2) declares these registers `volatile u_char' and byte-accesses them
              -- a reading, not a measurement.

    ONE BIT PER BLOCK, twelve, per instance: a driver bringing up the chip touches
    several of these in one pass, and one latch would announce the first and silence
    the rest (#497's own reasoning).  Auto-incremented rather than `1 << N' literals,
    which this fork records as collision-prone; PCC2_W_NBITS names the count, and the
    typedef after the enum makes the build fail if it ever exceeds the 32-bit field.

    REACHABILITY, verified: every non-byte m88k access is alignment-checked BEFORE
    memory_rw, and `relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE' keeps parity, so only the SIX
    EVEN-offset blocks (GENCTL, TxCTL, TxICR, SCCERR, SCCTX, IPL) are GUEST-reachable
    (this said "five" until pass 2; IPL at 0x3e is even, and a seat counted); the
    six odd ones -- VECBASE, PSCALEADJ, the T1 labels, SCCICR, SCCRX, SCSIICR, MASK
    -- are OPERATOR-reachable -- the debugger's `put h' warns on a
    misaligned address and stores anyway, MEASURED (artifacts/pcc2widthkills/
    operator_reach_20260903.out: `put h 0xfff42003' ended the host).  All twelve are
    converted; the detector reaches the odd ones through that path.

    NOT FIXED HERE, cited so "twelve kills removed" is not read as "file clean":
    pcc2c040kill (a LEGAL byte write of 0x04 to GENCTL still ends the host in
    reassert_interrupts) and pcc2defaultkill (an unlisted offset still ends it).  */
enum {
	PCC2_W_GENCTL_LEN,
	PCC2_W_VECBASE_LEN,
	PCC2_W_PSCALEADJ_LEN,
	PCC2_W_TXCTL_LEN,	/*  one block serves T1CTL and T2CTL  */
	PCC2_W_TXICR_LEN,	/*  one block serves T1ICR and T2ICR  */
	PCC2_W_SCCERR_LEN,
	PCC2_W_SCCICR_LEN,
	PCC2_W_SCCTX_LEN,
	PCC2_W_SCCRX_LEN,
	PCC2_W_SCSIICR_LEN,
	PCC2_W_IPL_LEN,		/*  was a #119 function-static: process-global  */
	PCC2_W_MASK_LEN,	/*  was a #119 function-static: process-global  */
	PCC2_W_NBITS
};
/*  A seat pointed out that NBITS "bounded" nothing.  Now it does: negative array
    size is a compile-time error, C99, no assert.h.  */
typedef char pcc2_w_fits_in_warned[PCC2_W_NBITS <= 32 ? 1 : -1];

struct pcc2_data {
	struct interrupt	cpu_irq;

	uint8_t			pcctwo_reg[PCC2_SIZE];

	/*  pcc2widthkills: one bit per width-guard block, see the enum.  Zeroed by
	    the init memset, so every instance starts with every announce armed.  */
	uint32_t		warned;

	uint8_t			cur_int_vec[8];

	/*  Timers:  */
	struct timer		*timer;
	int			pending_timer1_interrupts;
	int			pending_timer2_interrupts;
};


static uint32_t read32(unsigned char *p)
{
	uint32_t x = 0;
	size_t i;

	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
		x <<= 8;
		x |= p[i];
	}

	return x;
}


static void write32(unsigned char *p, uint32_t x)
{
	size_t i;

	for (i=0; i<sizeof(x); i++) {
		p[sizeof(x) - 1 - i] = x;
		x >>= 8;
	}
}


static void pcc_timer_tick(struct timer *t, void *extra)
{
	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;

	/*  The PCC2 timers run at 1 MHz.  */
	int steps = (int) (1000000.0 / PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ);

	uint32_t count1, count2, compare1, compare2;
	int interrupt1 = 0, interrupt2 = 0;

	/*  Read values:  */
	count1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT]);
	count2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT]);
	compare1 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CMP]);
	compare2 = read32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CMP]);

	/*  Timer enabled? Then count...  */
	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
		uint32_t old_count1 = count1;
		count1 += steps;

		/*  Did we pass the compare value?  */
		if ((old_count1 < compare1 && count1 >= compare1) ||
		    (old_count1 < compare1 && count1 < old_count1)) {
			interrupt1 = 1;

			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
				while (count1 >= compare1) {
					count1 -= compare1;
					interrupt1 ++;
				}
				interrupt1 --;
			}
		}

		/*  Overflow?  */
		if ((int32_t) old_count1 >= 0 && (int32_t) count1 < 0) {
			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] >> 4);
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] |= (oc << 4);
		}
	}

	/*  ... and the same for timer 2:  */
	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_CEN) {
		uint32_t old_count2 = count2;
		count2 += steps;

		if ((old_count2 < compare2 && count2 >= compare2) ||
		    (old_count2 < compare2 && count2 < old_count2)) {
			interrupt2 = 1;
			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] & PCC2_TCTL_COC) {
				while (count2 >= compare2) {
					count2 -= compare2;
					interrupt2 ++;
				}
				interrupt2 --;
			}
		}

		if ((int32_t) old_count2 >= 0 && (int32_t) count2 < 0) {
			int oc = 1 + (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] >> 4);
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] &= ~PCC2_TCTL_OVF;
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2CTL] |= (oc << 4);
		}
	}

	/*  Should we cause interrupts?  */
	if (interrupt1 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
		d->pending_timer1_interrupts += interrupt1;
	if (interrupt2 && d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
		d->pending_timer2_interrupts += interrupt2;

	/*  Write back values:  */
	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT], count1);
	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT], count2);
}


static void reassert_interrupts(struct pcc2_data *d)
{
	int assert = 0;

	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] & PCC2_C040) {
		/*  The M68000 interrupt mechanism involves outputting
		    interrupt level on pins EIPL<2..0>. Not implemented
		    yet.  */
		fatal("pcc2: C040 interrupt assertions... TODO\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	/*
	 *  Recalculate current interrupt level:
	 */
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = 0;

	/*  Timer interrupts?  */
	if (d->pending_timer1_interrupts > 0 &&
	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;
	if (d->pending_timer2_interrupts > 0 &&
	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN)
		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] |= PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;

	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER1;
		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
	}
	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT) {
		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2ICR] & PCC2_TTIRQ_IL;
		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_TIMER2;
		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
	}

	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_TX;
		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
	}

	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCC_RX;
		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
	}

	if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IEN &&
	    d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_INT) {
		int intlevel = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] & PCC2_IRQ_IPL;
		d->cur_int_vec[intlevel] = PCC2V_SCSI;
		if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] < intlevel)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] = intlevel;
	}

	/*  TODO: Other interrupt sources.  */

	/*  Assert interrupt on the CPU if the IPL is higher than the mask:  */
	if ((d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK) >
	    (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK))
		assert = 1;

	debug("[ pcc2: IPL = %i, MASK = %i => %s ]\n", d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL]& INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_MASK] & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK,
		assert ? "ASSERT" : "no assert");

	/*  ... but only allow interrupts if Master Interrupt Enable is on:  */
	if (!(d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] & PCC2_MIEN))
		assert = 0;

	if (assert)
		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->cpu_irq);
	else
		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->cpu_irq);
}


void pcctwo_interrupt_common(struct interrupt *interrupt, int assert)
{
	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) interrupt->extra;

	switch (interrupt->line) {

	case PCC2V_SCSI:
		if (assert)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		else
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCSIICR] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		break;

	case PCC2V_SCC_TX:
		if (assert)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		else
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCTX] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		break;

	case PCC2V_SCC_RX:
		if (assert)
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] |= PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		else
			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		break;

	default:fatal("[ pcctwo_interrupt_common: TODO: line = %i ]\n",
		    interrupt->line);
		exit(1);
	}

	reassert_interrupts(d);
}


static void pcctwo_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	pcctwo_interrupt_common(interrupt, 1);
}
static void pcctwo_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
{
	pcctwo_interrupt_common(interrupt, 0);
}


DEVICE_TICK(pcc2)
{
	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;
	reassert_interrupts(d);
}


/*  pcc2widthkills: decline a wide WRITE to a byte register, announcing once per
    block per instance.  The caller `break's after this; the read side of the same
    block never reaches here.  */
static void pcc2_width_decline(struct pcc2_data *d, int bit, const char *name, int len)
{
	if (!(d->warned & (1u << bit))) {
		d->warned |= (1u << bit);
		fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented len=%i write to %s -- declined."
		    "  (once) ]\n", len, name);
	}
}

DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2)
{
	uint64_t idata = 0;
	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;

	/*  0xfff42000..0xfff42fff, but only 0x40 unique registers:  */
	relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
		if (relative_addr + len <= PCC2_SIZE)	/* OB-11: end-span */
			memcpy(data, d->pcctwo_reg + relative_addr, len);
		else {
			static int warned = 0;	/* #119: loud-once (course-correction) */
			if (!warned) {
				fatal("[ pcc2: read 0x%x+%i beyond reg space 0x%x; "
				    "zero-filled ]\n", (int)relative_addr, (int)len,
				    (int)PCC2_SIZE);
				warned = 1;
			}
			memset(data, 0, len);	/* OB-11b: don't leak uninit host memory */
		}
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case PCCTWO_CHIPID:
	case PCCTWO_CHIPREV:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			fatal("[ IGNORING write to read-only PCCTWO_CHIPID/"
			    "CHIPREV register ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_GENCTL:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_GENCTL_LEN, "PCCTWO_GENCTL", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata;
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_VECBASE:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_VECBASE_LEN, "PCCTWO_VECBASE", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata & 0xf0;
			if (idata & ~0xf0)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_VECBASE"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_T1CMP:
	case PCCTWO_T2CMP:
	case PCCTWO_T1COUNT:
	case PCCTWO_T2COUNT:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE &&
		    relative_addr + len <= PCC2_SIZE)	/* OB-11: end-span */
			memcpy(d->pcctwo_reg + relative_addr, data, len);
		break;

	case PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_PSCALEADJ_LEN, "PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			fatal("[ pcc2: write to PSCALEADJ ignored (value "
			    "0x%02x) ]\n", (int)idata);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_T2CTL:
	case PCCTWO_T1CTL:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_TXCTL_LEN, "PCCTWO_TxCTL", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*  PCC2_TCTL_CEN and PCC2_TCTL_COC:  */
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] &= 0xfc;
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] |= (idata & 3);

			if (idata & PCC2_TCTL_COVF)
				d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] &=
				    ~(PCC2_TCTL_OVF | PCC2_TCTL_COVF);

			if (idata & ~7)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_TxCTL"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_T2ICR:
	case PCCTWO_T1ICR:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_TXICR_LEN, "PCCTWO_TxICR", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] &= ~0x17;
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] |= (idata & 0x17);

			if (relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR &&
			    !(idata & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN))
				d->pending_timer1_interrupts = 0;
			if (relative_addr == PCCTWO_T2ICR &&
			    !(idata & PCC2_TTIRQ_IEN))
				d->pending_timer2_interrupts = 0;

			if (relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR && (idata & PCC2_TTIRQ_ICLR)
			    && d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT
			    && d->pending_timer1_interrupts > 0)
				d->pending_timer1_interrupts --;

			if (relative_addr == PCCTWO_T2ICR && (idata & PCC2_TTIRQ_ICLR)
			    && d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] & PCC2_TTIRQ_INT
			    && d->pending_timer2_interrupts > 0)
				d->pending_timer2_interrupts --;

			if (idata & PCC2_TTIRQ_ICLR)
				d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] &= ~PCC2_TTIRQ_INT;

			if (idata & 0xe0)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_TxICR"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);

			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCCERR:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCERR_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCERR", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = 0;	/*  clear  */
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCCICR:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCICR_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCICR", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata & 0x1f;
			if (idata & ~0x1f)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_SCCICR"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCCTX:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCTX_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCTX", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata & 0x1f;
			if (idata & ~0x1f)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_SCCTX"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCCRX:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCRX_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCRX", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata & 0xdf;
			if (idata & ~0xdf)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_SCCRX"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCCRXIACK:
		/*  TODO. Hm. Is this enough?  */
		d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_SCCRX] &= ~PCC2_IRQ_INT;
		reassert_interrupts(d);
		break;

	case PCCTWO_SCSIICR:
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCSIICR_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCSIICR", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata & 0xdf;
			if (idata & ~0xdf)
				fatal("[ pcc2: HUH? write to PCCTWO_SCSIICR"
				    " with value 0x%02x. ]\n", (int) idata);
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_IPL:
		/*  pcc2widthkills: was a #119 `static int w' -- process-global, and it
		    said "ignored" about a READ the memcpy above had already served.
		    Now per-instance, write-only, like the ten blocks above.  */
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_IPL_LEN, "PCCTWO_IPL", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			static int w = 0;	/* #119: was exit(1) (IPL is read-only) */
			if (!w) { fatal("[ pcc2: write to read-only PCCTWO_IPL; "
			    "ignored ]\n"); w = 1; }
		} else {
			if (d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_IPL] == 0) {
				static int w = 0;	/* #119: was exit(1) */
				if (!w) { fatal("[ pcc2: IPL == 0 on read ]\n");
				    w = 1; }
			}
		}
		break;

	case PCCTWO_MASK:
		/*  pcc2widthkills: was a #119 `static int w' -- process-global, and it
		    said "ignored" about a READ the memcpy above had already served.
		    Now per-instance, write-only, like the ten blocks above.  */
		if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);
			break;
		}
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr] = idata;
			reassert_interrupts(d);
		}
		break;

	default:
		debug("[ pcc2: unimplemented %s offset 0x%x",
		    writeflag == MEM_WRITE? "write to" : "read from",
		    (int) relative_addr);
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			debug(": 0x%x", (int)idata);
		debug(" ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	return 1;
}


DEVICE_ACCESS(mvme187_iack)
{
	uint64_t odata = 0;
	struct pcc2_data *d = (struct pcc2_data *) extra;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
		fatal("[ pcc2: write to mvme187_iack? ]\n");
	} else {
		odata = d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] + d->cur_int_vec[
		    (relative_addr >> 2) & INTERRUPT_LEVEL_MASK];
		debug("[ pcc2: mvme187_iack level %i => vector 0x%02x ]\n",
		    (int)(relative_addr >> 2), (int)odata);

		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
	}

	return 1;
}


DEVINIT(pcc2)
{
	struct pcc2_data *d;
	int i;

	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct pcc2_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct pcc2_data)));
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct pcc2_data));

	/*
	 *  Initial values, according to the manual:
	 *
	 *  VECBASE is 0x0f after a reset, although the lowest four bits
	 *  cannot be manually written to after startup.
	 */
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPID] = PCC2_ID;
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_CHIPREV] = 0x00;
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_GENCTL] = 0x00;
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] = 0x0f;
	d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_PSCALEADJ] = 256 - 33;	// 256 - MHz (fake)

	/*  Connect to the CPU's interrupt pin:  */
	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->cpu_irq);

	/*
	 *  Register the 16 PCC2 interrupt vectors:
	 */
	for (i=0; i<16; i++) {
		struct interrupt templ;
		char n[300];
		snprintf(n, sizeof(n), "%s.pcc2.%i",
		    devinit->interrupt_path, i);

		memset(&templ, 0, sizeof(templ));
		templ.line = i;
		templ.name = n;
		templ.extra = d;
		templ.interrupt_assert = pcctwo_interrupt_assert;
		templ.interrupt_deassert = pcctwo_interrupt_deassert;

		interrupt_handler_register(&templ);
	}

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "pcc2",
	    devinit->addr, 4096, dev_pcc2_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "mvme187_iack",
	    M187_IACK, 32, dev_mvme187_iack_access, (void *)d,
	    DM_DEFAULT, NULL);

	d->timer = timer_add(PCC_TIMER_TICK_HZ, pcc_timer_tick, d);

	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
	    dev_pcc2_tick, d, DEV_PCC2_TICK_SHIFT);

	return 1;
}



 succeeded in 252ms:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""pcc2widthkills DETECTOR -- the twelve width-guard blocks in DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2).

WHAT THE FIX DID.  Twelve `if (len != 1) { fatal(...); exit(1); }' blocks (ten kills and
two #119 function-static latches) ended the host on a guest-chosen access width.  The
tree's own header says PCC2 registers "can be accessed as bytes, two-bytes, or
four-bytes", so the width is LEGAL.  The fix is a DIRECTION SPLIT:

    READS   ACCEPT -- the memcpy above the switch already served them, raw bytes in
            address order, width-correct by construction.  The guard discarded a
            correct answer.
    WRITES  DECLINE + per-instance latched announce -- every write arm uses `idata',
            the big-endian-composed value, which is "the addressed byte" ONLY at
            len==1.  Deleting the guard would store the NEIGHBOUR's lane.

CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this detector is designed to catch:

  CATCHES
    * reinstatement of the kill at any of the SIX GUEST-reachable blocks (survival
      rows paired with a liveness observable, because exit status cannot tell
      "declined and quit" from "died with status 0");
    * *** pass 2: the two FOLDED-IN arms, IPL and MASK, had NO ROW -- twelve blocks
      converted, ten given rows, and the shipped contract did not say so.  A seat
      found it by sibling parity: MASK is GENCTL's twin and had none of GENCTL's rows.
      Repaired: IPL is EVEN and therefore the SIXTH guest-reachable block (survival +
      message rows); MASK is odd (operator rows) and gets GENCTL's readback. ***
    * reinstatement at any of the seven OPERATOR-reachable blocks, through the
      debugger's `put h' at an odd address -- MEASURED to warn and store and kill
      (artifacts/pcc2widthkills/operator_reach_20260903.out);
    * *** DELETE instead of decline on the write side -- the lane hazard.  `st.h
      0xAABB' to GENCTL must leave GENCTL unchanged; a bare deletion stores 0xBB,
      VECBASE's lane, because (uint8_t) idata is the HIGHEST-address byte ***;
    * an accidental per-lane compose (GENCTL would read 0xAA);
    * the value a wide READ composes -- lane order and width -- not merely survival;
    * loss of the latch in either direction: dropped `|=' floods (same site twice
      must announce ONCE), and one bit shared across blocks silences (two sites must
      announce TWICE);
    * the de-escalation clause: byte writes at the LEGAL width at every reachable
      arm still land, read back through each arm's own mask, with NO decline message.

  DOES NOT CATCH, stated so an escape is outside the contract rather than an argument
    * per-instance vs process-global latch state: mvme187 instantiates ONE pcc2, so no
      session can hold two instances.  The bitfield is per-instance by construction;
      nothing here measures it;
    * pcc2c040kill -- a LEGAL byte write of 0x04 to GENCTL still ends the host in
      reassert_interrupts().  Every GENCTL lane value here keeps bit 0x04 CLEAR so no
      row dies for that reason;
    * pcc2defaultkill -- an unlisted offset (0x06, 0x14, 0x18, 0x2c ...) still ends the
      host in `default:';
    * pcc2counter8 -- the counter arm's raw memcpy of an 8-byte write;
    * what real hardware does with the LANES of a wide write (not on disk);
    * the SCCERR word-write's stored value: the arm always stores 0, so a decline and
      an accept are value-identical there and the row's channel is the message.

Sentinel: PCC2W_PROBE_DONE
"""

import os, pty, re, select, sys, time

BIN = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "build/gxemul"
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "local"

DUMMY = "/tmp/pcc2_dummy.bin"
open(DUMMY, "wb").write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")

CODE = 0x00010000
SCRATCH = 0x00020000
PCC2 = 0xfff42000
#  the five GUEST-reachable blocks (even offsets) and the seven OPERATOR-reachable ones
GENCTL, T2CTL, T2ICR, SCCERR, SCCTX = 0x02, 0x16, 0x1a, 0x1c, 0x1e
VECBASE, PSCALEADJ, T1CTL, T1ICR, SCCICR, SCCRX, SCSIICR = 0x03, 0x15, 0x17, 0x1b, 0x1d, 0x1f, 0x2f
#  pcc2 pass 2: THE TWO FOLDED-IN ARMS HAD NO ROW AT ALL.  A seat found that the probe
#  touched no offset at 0x3e or 0x3f -- twelve blocks converted, ten given rows.  MASK
#  is GENCTL's byte-identical twin (store byte, reassert); GENCTL's null-repair was
#  caught by `rb genctl', MASK's by nothing, a 60/60 sweep.  AND IPL IS EVEN: it is the
#  SIXTH guest-reachable block, not a seventh operator one.
IPL, MASK = 0x3e, 0x3f
CHIPID = 0x00


def enc(op26, rd, rs1, imm):
    return (op26 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (rs1 << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)


#  r2 = PCC2 base, r3 = scratch, r4 = load target, r5 = store source, r6 = second source
#  ld.b / ld.h (0x07 / 0x06) SIGN-EXTEND: 0x82 would print as 0xffffff82.  Every row that
#  asserts a VALUE uses the unsigned forms ld.bu / ld.hu (0x03 / 0x02); the survival rows
#  only check exit status and keep the signed forms.
def LD_B(rd, off): return enc(0x07, rd, 2, off)
def LD_H(rd, off): return enc(0x06, rd, 2, off)
def LD_BU(rd, off): return enc(0x03, rd, 2, off)
def LD_HU(rd, off): return enc(0x02, rd, 2, off)
def LD_W(rd, off): return enc(0x05, rd, 2, off)
def ST_B(rs, off): return enc(0x0b, rs, 2, off)
def ST_H(rs, off): return enc(0x0a, rs, 2, off)
def ST_W(rs, off): return enc(0x09, rs, 2, off)
I_ST_SCR = enc(0x09, 5, 3, 0)          # st  r5,r3,0   scratch word
I_LD_SCR = enc(0x05, 4, 3, 0)          # ld  r4,r3,0
I_BR_SELF = 0xc0000000

SENTINEL = 0xdeadbeef
DEVSIG = "IGNORING write to read-only PCCTWO_CHIPID"
DECL = "-- declined.  (once) ]"
rows = []


def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    assert name not in [n for n, _ in rows], "DUPLICATE ROW NAME %r" % name
    assert len(name) <= 15, name
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-16s %-12s %-12s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
                                          "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok


def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
    pid, fd = pty.fork()
    if pid == 0:
        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "mvme88k", "-e", "mvme187", "-M", "64",
                       "0x10000:" + DUMMY])
        os._exit(127)
    buf = ""

    def rd(t):
        nonlocal buf
        end = time.time() + t
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
            if not r:
                continue
            try:
                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
                if not d:
                    return False
                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
            except OSError:
                return False
        return True

    alive = rd(settle)
    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
        try:
            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
        except OSError:
            alive = False
            break
        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25):
            alive = False
            break
    try:
        os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
        rd(0.4)
    except OSError:
        pass
    try:
        os.close(fd)
    except OSError:
        pass
    how = "nostatus"
    try:
        _, stat = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
        if os.WIFSIGNALED(stat):
            how = "sig%d" % os.WTERMSIG(stat)
        elif os.WIFEXITED(stat):
            how = "exit%d" % os.WEXITSTATUS(stat)
    except OSError:
        pass
    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive, how


def printed(t, reg):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16) if m else "absent"


def program(insns, at=CODE):
    #  `at' lets a session run TWO programs: a second sequence at the SAME address finds
    #  pc already there and the assignment is skipped (the Addr hazard, designed out in
    #  the sgi probes with Addr.take()).  Callers needing two pass at=CODE + 0x100.
    words = list(insns) + [I_BR_SELF, 0]
    cmds = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, w) for i, w in enumerate(words)]
    return cmds + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(insns)]


def setup(r5="0x5a5a5a5a", r6="0x0"):
    return ["r2=0x%x" % PCC2, "r3=0x%x" % SCRATCH, "r5=%s" % r5, "r6=%s" % r6,
            "r4=0x%x" % SENTINEL]


print("pcc2widthkills DETECTOR -- binary=%s label=%s" % (BIN, LABEL))
print("%-16s %-12s %-12s %-46s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ controls
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([I_ST_SCR, I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("live", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a",
    "RAM word round-trips through the same decode")
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([ST_B(5, CHIPID)]))
row("devsig", t.count(DEVSIG), 1, "a byte write to CHIPID makes the DEVICE speak")
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([I_ST_SCR, LD_B(4, GENCTL), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("byte rd", how, "exit0", "ld.b GENCTL: the legal width, serviced on both builds")
row("byte rd liv", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")

# --------------------------------------- survival pairs at the five reachable blocks
#  Each kill row is PAIRED with a positive post-access observable.
for nm, off in (("genctl", GENCTL), ("t2ctl", T2CTL), ("t2icr", T2ICR),
                ("sccerr", SCCERR), ("scctx", SCCTX)):
    t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([I_ST_SCR, LD_H(4, off), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
    row("hrd " + nm, how, "exit0", "ld.h %s (aligned halfword READ) survives" % nm.upper())
    row("hrd %s lv" % nm, printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
    #  r6 = the halfword to store: both lanes keep GENCTL's bit 0x04 CLEAR (pcc2c040kill)
    t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0xaabb") + program([I_ST_SCR, ST_H(6, off), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
    row("hwr " + nm, how, "exit0", "st.h %s (aligned halfword WRITE) survives" % nm.upper())
    row("hwr %s lv" % nm, printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
#  SCCERR is the one 4-aligned block: word read AND write
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([I_ST_SCR, LD_W(4, SCCERR), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("wrd sccerr", how, "exit0", "ld SCCERR (the one 4-aligned block) survives")
row("wrd sccerr lv", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0x00000000") + program([I_ST_SCR, ST_W(6, SCCERR), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("wwr sccerr", how, "exit0", "st SCCERR (word WRITE) survives")
row("wwr sccerr lv", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
#  SCCERR's arm always stores 0, so decline and accept are VALUE-identical there: the
#  only channel that varies is the announcement.
row("wwr sccerr dcl", t.count("write to PCCTWO_SCCERR " + DECL), 1,
    "...and it was DECLINED once -- the message is the only channel here")

# ---------------------------------------------------------- VALUE rows (reads)
#  A wide read must compose the lanes in ADDRESS order.  VECBASE's arm masks & 0xf0
#  and GENCTL's stores the byte, so the halfword at 0x02 is GENCTL<<8 | VECBASE.
#  0x82 keeps bit 0x04 clear.
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x82", r6="0x50")
                        + program([ST_B(5, GENCTL), ST_B(6, VECBASE), LD_HU(4, GENCTL)]) + ["print r4"])
row("val h genctl", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x8250",
    "ld.h at GENCTL composes GENCTL:VECBASE in address order -- kills lane-order / zero-fill")
#  word at SCCERR: SCCERR always 0; SCCICR & 0x1f; SCCTX & 0x1f; SCCRX & 0xdf.
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x15", r6="0x1f")
                        + program([ST_B(5, SCCICR), ST_B(6, SCCTX), ST_B(6, SCCRX), LD_W(4, SCCERR)]) + ["print r4"])
row("val w sccerr", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x151f1f",
    "ld at SCCERR composes 00:SCCICR:SCCTX:SCCRX -- four lanes, right order")

# ------------------------------------------------ READBACK rows (the DELETE killer)
#  After a declined st.h 0xAABB to GENCTL, BOTH lanes must be unchanged.  Under a bare
#  deletion GENCTL reads 0xBB (the neighbour's lane); under an accidental compose it
#  reads 0xAA and VECBASE 0xB0.  Both lanes of 0xAABB keep bit 0x04 clear.
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x82", r6="0xaabb")
                        + program([ST_B(5, GENCTL), ST_H(6, GENCTL), LD_BU(4, GENCTL)]) + ["print r4"])
row("rb genctl", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x82",
    "GENCTL unchanged after a declined st.h -- kills DELETE (0xbb) and compose (0xaa)")
row("rb genctl dcl", t.count("write to PCCTWO_GENCTL " + DECL), 1,
    "...and the decline was announced ONCE")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x50", r6="0xaabb")
                        + program([ST_B(5, VECBASE), ST_H(6, GENCTL), LD_BU(4, VECBASE)]) + ["print r4"])
row("rb vecbase", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x50",
    "VECBASE unchanged too -- the second lane of the declined halfword")

# ---------------------------- per-arm channels (pass 2: sibling parity, arm by arm)
#  A seat pointed out that only GENCTL had a readback: every other reachable arm's
#  halfword-write row checked SURVIVAL alone, so a guard edited at ONE arm (`len > 2',
#  or the guard deleted, at T2ICR only) left every row green.  Each arm now has its own
#  channel, with a lane value its own mask turns into a different byte when it lands.
for nm, off, pre, landed in (("t2ctl", T2CTL, "0x03", "0x3"), ("t2icr", T2ICR, "0x13", "0x13"),
                             ("scctx", SCCTX, "0x1f", "0x1f")):
    t, alive, how = session(setup(r5=pre, r6="0xaab8")
                            + program([ST_B(5, off), ST_H(6, off), LD_BU(4, off)]) + ["print r4"])
    row("rb " + nm, printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", landed,
        "%s unchanged after a declined st.h -- kills a guard edited at THIS arm" % nm.upper())
    row("rb %s dcl" % nm, t.count("write to PCCTWO_%s " % ("TxCTL" if nm == "t2ctl" else "TxICR" if nm == "t2icr" else "SCCTX") + DECL), 1,
        "...announced once at this arm")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0xaab8") + program([ST_H(6, SCCERR)]))
row("hwr sccerr dcl", t.count("write to PCCTWO_SCCERR " + DECL), 1,
    "halfword write to SCCERR declined once -- value-identical arm, message channel")

#  PRE-TABLE BLOCK yield (section 1, guard field vs body field): the guard reads the access
#  SHAPE (len) and the guarded body reads the VALUE (idata).  An operand swap to a value
#  test -- `idata > 0xff', a plausible "does it fit a byte" reading -- accepts a halfword
#  write whose value fits, and big-endian 0x0022 then stores 0x22, the NEIGHBOUR's lane,
#  into GENCTL: the original defect, behind a guard that still reads like a width check.
#  Every other wide-write row used a value above 0xff, so all 77 were green under it BY
#  CONSTRUCTION.  Written before the repair table ran; the table says whether it kills.
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x11", r6="0x0022")
                        + program([ST_B(5, GENCTL), ST_H(6, GENCTL), LD_BU(4, GENCTL)]) + ["print r4"])
row("rb genctl lo", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x11",
    "st.h of a value that FITS a byte is still declined -- the guard is on len, not idata")

# ------------------------- latch CARDINALITY (pass 2, the measuring seat's class closer)
#  `latch same' and `latch two' pin ONE pair out of 66: every other session writes at most
#  one BLOCK, so a bit shared by any other pair announces normally and no row moves.
#  MEASURED by the builder: aliasing SCCTX onto SCCRX's bit -- a ONE-CHARACTER edit --
#  left the 60-row detector at 60/60.  ONE OFFSET PER BLOCK: TxCTL serves T1CTL+T2CTL
#  and TxICR serves T1ICR+T2ICR, so twelve LABELS would give only ten announces -- the
#  same units slip that let "five even blocks + seven odd ones = twelve" read as all
#  twelve blocks.  This row pins the CARDINALITY of the latch: any collision anywhere in
#  the 12-bit space drops the count.  (Operator path for all twelve, so the odd ones are
#  reachable; the guest-reachable halves have their own rows above.)
BLOCKS12 = (("GENCTL", 0x02), ("VECBASE", 0x03), ("PSCALEADJ", 0x15), ("TxCTL", 0x16),
            ("TxICR", 0x1a), ("SCCERR", 0x1c), ("SCCICR", 0x1d), ("SCCTX", 0x1e),
            ("SCCRX", 0x1f), ("SCSIICR", 0x2f), ("IPL", 0x3e), ("MASK", 0x3f))
#  VALUE 0x0003, not 0x0303: it fits a byte, so this row ALSO reds if any arm swaps its
#  guard operand from len to a value test (see `rb genctl lo').  On the fix the value
#  never lands, so the expectation is value-independent.
t, alive, how = session(["put h 0x%x, 0x0003" % (PCC2 + o) for _, o in BLOCKS12])
row("latch all", t.count(DECL), 12,
    "one wide write per BLOCK -> TWELVE announces: bit CARDINALITY, not one pair")
row("latch all lv", how, "exit0", "...and the host survived all twelve blocks")

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ latch rows
t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0xaabb") + program([ST_H(6, GENCTL), ST_H(6, GENCTL)]))
row("latch same", t.count("write to PCCTWO_GENCTL " + DECL), 1,
    "same block twice -> ONE announce -- kills a dropped |= (flood)")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0x0303") + program([ST_H(6, GENCTL), ST_H(6, T2CTL)]))
row("latch two", t.count(DECL), 2,
    "two blocks -> TWO announces -- kills a bit shared across blocks")

# --------------------------------------------- de-escalation ACCEPT rows (byte writes)
#  Removing twelve exit(1)s destroys the tripwire that announced guard growth.  So the
#  legal width must still LAND at each reachable arm, through its own mask, with no
#  decline message.  Expected values from the arms' own semantics.
for nm, off, wr, rdv in (("genctl", GENCTL, "0x82", "0x82"),     # stores the byte; bit 0x04 clear
                          ("t2ctl", T2CTL, "0x03", "0x3"),       # &= 0xfc; |= (v & 3)
                          ("t2icr", T2ICR, "0x13", "0x13"),      # &= ~0x17; |= (v & 0x17)
                          ("scctx", SCCTX, "0x1f", "0x1f"),      # = v & 0x1f
                          ("sccerr", SCCERR, "0x5a", "0x0")):    # any write clears to 0
    t, alive, how = session(setup(r5=wr) + program([ST_B(5, off), LD_BU(4, off)]) + ["print r4"])
    row("acc " + nm, printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", rdv,
        "st.b %s at the legal width lands through its own mask" % nm.upper())
    row("acc %s nod" % nm, t.count(DECL), 0, "...with NO decline message")

# ------------------------------- OPERATOR path: the seven odd blocks via `put h'
#  The m88k alignment check makes these guest-unreachable; the debugger warns on a
#  misaligned put and stores anyway, straight to the handler (MEASURED pre-fix: exit1).
for nm, off in (("vecbase", VECBASE), ("pscale", PSCALEADJ), ("t1ctl", T1CTL),
                ("t1icr", T1ICR), ("sccicr", SCCICR), ("sccrx", SCCRX), ("scsiicr", SCSIICR)):
    t, alive, how = session(["put h 0x%x, 0x0303" % (PCC2 + off)])
    row("op " + nm, how, "exit0", "debugger put h at odd %s survives" % nm.upper())
    row("op %s dcl" % nm, t.count(DECL), 1, "...and was declined once")

#  --------- the odd arms' FALL-THROUGH channel (pass 3, ruled a floor breach, not a disclosure)
#  The block above proves each odd arm ANNOUNCES; it cannot see a guard that announces AND
#  then lets the halfword land (the M_FALL shape -- decline nulled, guard kept: clause (d)).
#  PRETABLE Section 2 had disclosed that as "a sentence owed".  The measuring seat asked
#  whether a disclosure can discharge a floor clause; it cannot, or every breach becomes a
#  sentence.  So each of the four arms gets the channel its body offers:
#    SCCICR/SCCRX/SCSIICR store `idata & mask' then reassert_interrupts(): set 0x1f by
#      byte (legal under both masks), decline a wide 0xaab8, read the byte back -- a landed
#      write leaves 0x18 (& 0x1f) or 0x98 (& 0xdf) in an INTERRUPT CONTROL register.
#    PSCALEADJ stores nothing; its write body only prints "ignored (value ...)".  A landed
#      wide write therefore prints that line -- the message is the only channel, and the
#      row counts it at ZERO.
for nm, off in (("sccicr", SCCICR), ("sccrx", SCCRX), ("scsiicr", SCSIICR)):
    t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x1f") + program([ST_B(5, off)])
                            + ["put h 0x%x, 0xaab8" % (PCC2 + off)]
                            + program([LD_BU(4, off)], at=CODE + 0x100) + ["print r4"])
    row("rb " + nm, printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x1f",
        "%s unchanged after a declined put h -- a fall-through lands 0x18/0x98 and reasserts" % nm.upper())
t, alive, how = session(["put h 0x%x, 0x0303" % (PCC2 + PSCALEADJ)])
row("op pscale noig", t.count("PSCALEADJ ignored"), 0,
    "a declined wide write never reaches the 'ignored' print -- fall-through would")

# --------------------------------- IPL (guest-reachable, EVEN) and MASK (operator, odd)
#  IPL 0x3e: a halfword at 0x3e lands in the IPL arm.  Its write side is read-only (#119
#  "ignored"), so a wide write is declined by the WIDTH latch first and a readback cannot
#  tell the two refusals apart -- the channel is the message.
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([I_ST_SCR, LD_H(4, IPL), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("hrd ipl", how, "exit0", "ld.h IPL (EVEN, the SIXTH guest-reachable block) survives")
row("hrd ipl lv", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r6="0x1234") + program([I_ST_SCR, ST_H(6, IPL), I_LD_SCR]) + ["print r4"])
row("hwr ipl", how, "exit0", "st.h IPL survives")
row("hwr ipl lv", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x5a5a5a5a", "...and runs on")
row("hwr ipl dcl", t.count("write to PCCTWO_IPL " + DECL), 1,
    "...declined by the WIDTH latch (not the read-only one) -- the message is the channel")
#  MASK 0x3f: odd, operator path.  It is GENCTL's twin, so it gets GENCTL's readback: set
#  MASK to a known byte at the legal width, decline a wide write through the debugger,
#  then read the byte back through a program at a SECOND address.  A deleted guard stores
#  the wrong lane (0xbb); an accidental compose stores 0xaa.  reassert_interrupts on a
#  MASK byte write is harmless here: GENCTL's C040 bit is clear and nothing is pending.
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x12") + program([ST_B(5, MASK)])
                        + ["put h 0x%x, 0xaabb" % (PCC2 + MASK)]
                        + program([LD_BU(4, MASK)], at=CODE + 0x100) + ["print r4"])
row("op mask", how, "exit0", "debugger put h at odd MASK survives")
row("op mask dcl", t.count("write to PCCTWO_MASK " + DECL), 1, "...and was declined once")
row("rb mask", printed(t, "r4") if alive else "died", "0x12",
    "MASK unchanged after the declined wide write -- GENCTL's twin gets GENCTL's readback")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------- summary
print("NOROW  two device instances               -- mvme187 has ONE pcc2; per-instance latch state"
      " is by construction, not measured")
print("NOROW  pcc2c040kill                        -- a LEGAL byte write of 0x04 to GENCTL still ends"
      " the host; every GENCTL lane here keeps that bit clear")
print("NOROW  pcc2defaultkill / pcc2counter8      -- other arms, other rounds")
bad = [n for n, ok in rows if not ok]
print("PCC2W_RESULT=%d/%d" % (len(rows) - len(bad), len(rows)))
print("PCC2W_LABEL=%s" % LABEL)
if bad:
    print("PCC2W_RED=%s" % ",".join(bad))
print("PCC2W_PROBE_DONE")

OUT=/tmp/killpcc2b2; PROBE=pcc2_width_probe.py
say() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "*** ABORT: $*"; exit 9; }
rm -rf "$T" "$OUT"; mkdir -p "$OUT"
cp -a "$G/build" "$T" || die "clone failed"
cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || { cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; say "  clone's $F refreshed from SEC (comment-only change since build/ was built)"; }

build() { cd "$T" || die cd; rm -f src/devices/dev_pcc2.o; make -j4 > "$OUT/build_$1.log" 2>&1; [ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$T/$F" ] || die "$1: binary not newer than source"; }
score() {
    cd "$SEC/regress" || die cd
    python3 -u "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" "$T/gxemul" "$1" > "$OUT/$1.out" 2>&1
    grep -q PCC2W_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/$1.out" || die "$1: NO COMPLETION SENTINEL"
    say "  $1 [$2]: $(grep -m1 PCC2W_RESULT "$OUT/$1.out")"
    grep -m1 PCC2W_RED "$OUT/$1.out" | sed 's/PCC2W_RED=/      RED: /' || true
}
mut() {
    echo "$F  mutant $1" > "$T/.MUTANT"; cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"
    python3 - "$T/$F" "$3" <<'PYEOF'
import io, sys
p, body = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]; s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()
ns = {'s': s}; exec(body, ns); io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(ns['s'])
PYEOF
    [ $? -eq 0 ] || die "$1: mutator failed"
    cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "$1: file UNCHANGED after mutation"
    build "$1"; score "$1" "$2"
}

#!/bin/bash
#  pcc2widthkills pass-2 repair table -- the IPL/MASK rows.  Runs after the panels clear.
#    BASE          78/78 owed
#    M_DELETE_MASK MASK guard removed        -> rb mask reads 0xbb (the twin of M_DELETE)   was a 60/60 sweep
#    M_KILL_IPL    IPL width kill reinstated -> hrd ipl exit1 (GUEST-reachable, even)       was a 60/60 sweep
#    M_KILL_MASK   MASK width kill reinstated-> op mask exit1 (operator)                   was a 60/60 sweep
#    M_DELETE      (control) still reds rb genctl -- the repair perturbed nothing
#  PER-ARM (pass 2, grok): only GENCTL had a readback, so a guard edited at ONE arm escaped.
#    M_DELETE_TXICR  TXICR guard removed      -> rb t2icr 0x10 (halfword lands: 0xaab8 & 0x17)
#    M_LENGT2_TXICR  `len != 1' -> `len > 2'  -> rb t2icr 0x10, rb t2icr dcl 0
#    M_LENGT2_TXCTL  same at TXCTL            -> rb t2ctl 0x0  (0xaab8 & 3)
#    M_LENGT2_SCCTX  same at SCCTX            -> rb scctx 0x18 (0xaab8 & 0x1f)
#    M_LENGT2_SCCERR same at SCCERR           -> hwr sccerr dcl 0 (value-identical arm: message only)
#  THE BUILDER'S SWEEPS not already above (Opus, pass 2, table of 11):
#    M_ALIAS_SCCTX   SCCTX declines on SCCRX's bit (ONE char) -> latch all 11 (a collision drops the count)
#    M_LENGT2_MASK   MASK `len != 1' -> `len > 2'        -> rb mask 0xbb (halfword lands)
#    M_FALL_MASK     MASK announces then falls through   -> rb mask 0xbb
#    M_IDATA_GENCTL guard operand len -> idata > 0xff (PRE-TABLE yield) -> rb genctl lo 0x22, latch all 11
set -u
G=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL; SEC=$G/GXEMUL-SEC; T=/tmp/gxpcc2b; F=src/devices/dev_pcc2.c
OUT=/tmp/killpcc2b; PROBE=pcc2_width_probe.py
say() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "*** ABORT: $*"; exit 9; }
rm -rf "$T" "$OUT"; mkdir -p "$OUT"
cp -a "$G/build" "$T" || die "clone failed"
cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || { cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; say "  clone's $F refreshed from SEC (comment-only change since build/ was built)"; }
cp "$SEC/regress/$PROBE" "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze probe"
say "  instrument frozen: $(wc -c < "$OUT/frozen_probe.py") bytes"
build() { cd "$T" || die cd; rm -f src/devices/dev_pcc2.o; make -j4 > "$OUT/build_$1.log" 2>&1; [ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$T/$F" ] || die "$1: binary not newer than source"; }
score() {
    cd "$SEC/regress" || die cd
    python3 -u "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" "$T/gxemul" "$1" > "$OUT/$1.out" 2>&1
    grep -q PCC2W_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/$1.out" || die "$1: NO COMPLETION SENTINEL"
    say "  $1 [$2]: $(grep -m1 PCC2W_RESULT "$OUT/$1.out")"
    grep -m1 PCC2W_RED "$OUT/$1.out" | sed 's/PCC2W_RED=/      RED: /' || true
}
mut() {
    echo "$F  mutant $1" > "$T/.MUTANT"; cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"
    python3 - "$T/$F" "$3" <<'PYEOF'
import io, sys
p, body = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]; s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()
ns = {'s': s}; exec(body, ns); io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(ns['s'])
PYEOF
    [ $? -eq 0 ] || die "$1: mutator failed"
    cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "$1: file UNCHANGED after mutation"
    build "$1"; score "$1" "$2"
}

#!/bin/bash
#  PASS 3 -- the repair made in response to my pass 2 owes its own pass 2.
#  Re-runs my SIX clean sweeps against the REPAIRED 78-row detector.
#  Instrument FROZEN (probe.frozen.py) so all seven runs share one instrument.
T=/tmp/gxopus_pcc2
OUT=/mnt/c/Users/User00/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--DocumentNoSnc-CC-GXEMUL/8e474f41-4d47-4c99-bf11-3406a84c4fcd/scratchpad/pcc2p2_opus/pass3
BASE2=$OUT/dev_pcc2.base2.c
PROBE=$OUT/probe.frozen.py
SRC=$T/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c
build () {
	rm -f "$T/src/devices/dev_pcc2.o" "$T/gxemul"
	( cd "$T" && make ) > /tmp/pcc2_build.log 2>&1
	[ -x "$T/gxemul" ] || { echo "  BUILD FAILED"; grep -m5 -E 'error' /tmp/pcc2_build.log; return 1; }
	[ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$SRC" ] || { echo "  *** STALE BINARY ***"; return 1; }
	echo "  build ok, binary -nt source"; return 0
}
runprobe () {
	( cd /mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress \
	  && timeout 1800 python3 -u "$PROBE" "$T/gxemul" "$1" ) > "$OUT/p3_$1.out" 2>&1
	grep -E '^PCC2W_(RESULT|RED|PROBE_DONE)' "$OUT/p3_$1.out" | sed 's/^/  /'
	grep -q PCC2W_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/p3_$1.out" || echo "  *** NO SENTINEL ***"
}
mutate () {
	python3 - "$1" <<'PYEOF'
import sys
tag = sys.argv[1]; BS = chr(92)
p = "/tmp/gxopus_pcc2/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c"
s = open(p).read()
G = "if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {"
def one(old, new):
    global s
    n = s.count(old); assert n == 1, "COUNT %d for %r" % (n, old[:70])
    s = s.replace(old, new)
if tag == "BASE2":
    pass
elif tag == "M1":
    one('pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCTX_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCTX", len);',
        'pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCRX_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCTX", len);')
elif tag == "M2":
    one(G + '\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);',
        'if (len > 2 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);')
elif tag == "M6":
    one('pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;',
        'pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);')
elif tag == "M7":
    one(G + '\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_IPL_LEN, "PCCTWO_IPL", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;',
        'if (len != 1) {\n\t\t\tfatal("TODO: pcc2: non-byte access to PCCTWO_IPL' + BS + 'n");\n\t\t\texit(1);')
elif tag == "M8":
    one('\t\t' + G + '\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_SCCTX_LEN, "PCCTWO_SCCTX", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t}\n', '')
elif tag == "M9":
    one('\t\t' + G + '\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_MASK_LEN, "PCCTWO_MASK", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t}\n', '')
else:
    raise SystemExit("unknown " + tag)
open(p, "w").write(s)
print("  mutated: " + tag)
PYEOF
}
echo "=== PASS 3: six sweeps vs the REPAIRED 78-row detector ==="; date
for M in BASE2 M1 M2 M6 M7 M8 M9; do
	echo; echo "### $M"
	echo "$M dev_pcc2.c" > "$T/.MUTANT"
	cp "$BASE2" "$SRC"
	if mutate "$M"; then if build; then runprobe "$M"; fi; fi
done

## FINDING FROM READING, INDEPENDENT OF THE TABLE: THE OWED SENTENCE WAS NOT WRITTEN

The repair's own PRETABLE (SECTION 2) says, of PSCALEADJ/SCCICR/SCCRX/SCSIICR:

   of those four would escape; THAT IS THE SENTENCE OWED"

Measured against the shipped probe:

  CATCHES list        b78bb0f 19 lines -> repaired 24 lines   (GREW)
  DOES NOT CATCH      byte-IDENTICAL to b78bb0f               (UNCHANGED)
  the owed sentence   appears NOWHERE in pcc2_width_probe.py

So the repair strengthened its POSITIVE claims and did not record the NEGATIVE one its
own artifact named.  This matters under the round's OWN reading of clause (e): a
disclosed escape is "outside the contract" only if the CONTRACT says so.  The
disclosure lives in a kill-table artifact; the contract is what ships beside the rows
and is what a future reader consults.  A contract that grew its CATCHES list while its
DOES-NOT-CATCH list stood still reads as MORE complete than before, not less.

Disposition: L3 (undischarged obligation -- the round's own artifact names it as owed)
or L4 (wrong record).  Cost: one sentence.  Independent of how the floor question below
is resolved.

## LATCH-AXIS ROBUSTNESS OF THE REPAIR (reasoned, not measured -- stated as such)

`latch all' counts DECL occurrences == 12 across twelve one-write-per-block sessions.
Single-edit attacks on the latch and where they land:

  drop `d->warned |= bit'  -> every block floods, but `latch all' writes each block
                             ONCE so it still counts 12.  CAUGHT BY `latch same' (2 != 1).
  invert the latch test    -> nothing ever announces; `latch all' 0.  CAUGHT.
  alias two enum members   -> 11.  CAUGHT (measured: M1).
  change the message text  -> DECL stops matching; count drops.  CAUGHT.
  duplicate a decline call -> latch still prints once; 12.  Benign (no behaviour change).

So `latch all' + `latch same' cover the latch axis between them: cardinality catches
collisions, same-site-twice catches floods.  Neither alone does.

A read-side guard regression scoped to an ODD arm (e.g. `len != 1' restored for BOTH
directions at SCCICR) has no row -- but it is nearly harmless: the memcpy above the
switch has already served the data, so the only effect is a spurious announce whose
text says "write" about a read.  Recorded as benign, not filed.
M1: latch all 11/12 FAIL -- sole killer as predicted

MEASURED, pass 3 (78-row repaired detector, frozen instrument):
  BASE2  78/78
  M1     77/78  RED: latch all            <- prediction was "latch all SOLE killer": EXACT

   zero sweeps ... Owes pass 2".

## WHY M6 + PASS 3b TOGETHER MAKE THE ARGUMENT, rather than either alone

From the round's own part1 reds:
  M_KILL_IPL      latch all, latch all lv, hrd ipl, hrd ipl lv, hwr ipl, hwr ipl lv, hwr ipl dcl
  M_DELETE_MASK   latch all, op mask dcl, rb mask
  M_LENGT2_SCCTX  rb scctx, rb scctx dcl, latch all

Every one of those shapes makes the arm STOP ANNOUNCING, so the `dcl' row and
`latch all' both move -- two independent channels.  The ANNOUNCE-THEN-LAND shape is
different: the announce SURVIVES, so `dcl' stays 1 and `latch all' stays 12, and the
READBACK is the only channel left.

  M6 (my run)   = M_FALL_MASK at an arm that HAS a readback  -> expect RED on `rb mask' ALONE
  PASS 3b       = the same shape at the four arms WITHOUT one -> expect 78/78

If both land as predicted, the pair is the proof: the shape is detectable ONLY by a
readback, and four of the twelve arms have none.  That is a statement about the
detector's structure, not about one lucky mutant.
  M2   75/78  RED: latch all, op mask dcl, rb mask   <- all three predicted killers

## THE DECISIVE OBSERVATION, measured mid-M6

Under M6 (announce-then-land at MASK) the cardinality row reads:

    latch all        12           12    ... ok
    latch all lv     exit0        exit0 ... ok

`latch all' is GREEN.  It must be: the decline helper is still CALLED, so the bit is
still set and the message still printed; only the `break' is gone, so the write ALSO
lands.  Cardinality counts ANNOUNCES, and the announce is intact.

So for the announce-then-land shape the ONLY available channel is a READBACK of the
register.  MASK has one (`rb mask').  PSCALEADJ, SCCICR, SCCRX and SCSIICR do not --
which is exactly what the repair's PRETABLE discloses and what pass 3b measures.

This is clause (f)'s lesson in a new costume: a suite that asserts the ANNOUNCE
(distinctness/cardinality) is invariant under an edit that leaves the announce alone.

  M6   77/78  RED: rb mask ALONE   <- prediction EXACT.  latch all 12 (green),
                                      op mask dcl 1 (green).  The READBACK is the only
                                      channel that sees announce-then-land.

## THE STRUCTURAL RESULT, now measured rather than argued

  shape                         latch all   <arm> dcl   readback
  guard deleted / len>2 / kill   MOVES       MOVES       moves
  ANNOUNCE-THEN-LAND             12 (green)  1 (green)   THE ONLY CHANNEL

Twelve blocks; readbacks exist at GENCTL, VECBASE, TxCTL, TxICR, SCCTX, MASK (and
SCCERR is value-identical by design, disclosed).  PSCALEADJ, SCCICR, SCCRX, SCSIICR
have NONE -- so the announce-then-land shape at those four has no channel at all.
Pass 3b measures exactly that.

  round's rig  /tmp/gxpcc2b     their driver, their clone   BASE 78/78
  my rig       /tmp/gxopus_pcc2 my driver, my clone         BASE2 78/78

and the overlapping cell agrees channel-for-channel:

  my    M2             75/78  latch all, op mask dcl, rb mask

Two independently built binaries, two drivers, two clones, one detector, same answer.
That is the control neither table has on its own.
  M7   71/78  RED: latch all, latch all lv, hrd ipl, hrd ipl lv, hwr ipl, hwr ipl lv,
                    hwr ipl dcl   -- IDENTICAL to the round's own M_KILL_IPL (71/78,

                    different edits, same observable, same channels.
  M9   75/78  RED: latch all, op mask dcl, rb mask  -- same three channels as M2 (both

every red row was predicted by name before the run (PREDICTIONS3.txt, 13:41).

## PASS 3b -- THE DISCLOSED GAP WAS REAL

  M_FALL_SCCICR  vs the 78-row detector:  78/78  TOTAL CLEAN SWEEP
  (predicted 78/78 at 13:51, before the cell ran)

Guard kept, announce kept, `break' removed.  `op sccicr dcl' stays 1 and `latch all'
stays 12 BY CONSTRUCTION, and 0xb8 & 0x1f = 0x18 lands in SCCICR -- an INTERRUPT
CONTROL register -- followed by reassert_interrupts().  Every one of the 78 rows green.

So the PRETABLE's own sentence ("a guard edit that lands the write AND still announces
at one of those four would escape") was a CORRECT prediction of a real clause-(d)
breach, not a pessimistic disclaimer.  Disclosing it would have shipped it.

That settles the disposition question empirically in favour of the reading the round
adopted: a floor clause cannot be discharged by a sentence.  The four rows added in the
78 -> 82 step were NECESSARY, and pass 3c now checks that they actually kill this.

#!/bin/bash
#  pcc2widthkills KILL TABLE -- runs AFTER the pass-15 table clears (one emulator at a time).
#
#  Sequence, each a checkpoint line:
#    0. PREFIX  -- the pcc2 detector against the COMMITTED build (pre-fix dev_pcc2.c):
#                  floor clause (a).  Predicted: every survival/op row red, controls green.
#    1. propagate SEC's dev_pcc2.c into the clone, rebuild, BASE must be 60/60.
#    2. mutants, each labelled with the row it must red:
#         M_DELETE   GENCTL guard removed outright         -> rb genctl reads 0xbb (lane hazard)
#         M_NOBREAK  decline announced, then falls through -> rb genctl reads 0xbb, rb genctl dcl 1
#         M_ASSIGN   latch never set                       -> latch same 2
#         M_SHARED   every block uses GENCTL's bit         -> latch two 1
#         M_RDKILL   read side killed again at GENCTL      -> hrd genctl exit1
#         M_OPKILL   VECBASE (odd, operator-reachable) killed again -> op vecbase exit1
#         M_COMPOSE  accidental per-lane store on GENCTL   -> rb genctl 0xaa, rb vecbase 0xb0
#    3. restore + rebuild + verify, clear the sentinel.
set -u
G=/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL; SEC=$G/GXEMUL-SEC; T=/tmp/gxpcc2; F=src/devices/dev_pcc2.c
OUT=/tmp/killpcc2; PROBE=pcc2_width_probe.py
say() { echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] $*"; }
die() { echo "*** ABORT: $*"; exit 9; }
rm -rf "$T" "$OUT"; mkdir -p "$OUT"
say "clone build/ -> $T (build/ still carries the PRE-fix dev_pcc2.c: that is the PREFIX cell)"
cp -a "$G/build" "$T" || die "clone failed"
cp "$SEC/regress/$PROBE" "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" || die "cannot freeze probe"
say "  instrument frozen: $(wc -c < "$OUT/frozen_probe.py") bytes"
#  build/ carries the FIXED file by the time this runs (it was rebuilt for the gate), so
#  the PREFIX cell installs HEAD's pre-fix copy into the clone explicitly, and says so.
git -C "$SEC" show HEAD:$F > "$OUT/dev_pcc2.PREFIX.c" || die "cannot extract HEAD's $F"
cp "$OUT/dev_pcc2.PREFIX.c" "$T/$F"
cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "PREFIX copy is identical to the fixed file -- HEAD already has the fix?"
say "  PREFIX: HEAD's pre-fix $F installed in the clone (differs from SEC, as it must)"

build() { cd "$T" || die cd; rm -f src/devices/dev_pcc2.o; make -j4 > "$OUT/build_$1.log" 2>&1; [ "$T/gxemul" -nt "$T/$F" ] || die "$1: binary not newer than source"; }
score() {
    cd "$SEC/regress" || die cd
    python3 -u "$OUT/frozen_probe.py" "$T/gxemul" "$1" > "$OUT/$1.out" 2>&1
    grep -q PCC2W_PROBE_DONE "$OUT/$1.out" || die "$1: NO COMPLETION SENTINEL"
    say "  $1 [$2]: $(grep -m1 PCC2W_RESULT "$OUT/$1.out")"
    grep -m1 PCC2W_RED "$OUT/$1.out" | sed 's/PCC2W_RED=/      RED: /' || true
}

say "PREFIX -- floor clause (a), the committed build"
build PREFIX; score PREFIX PREFIX

say "PROPAGATE the fix into the clone, rebuild, BASE"
cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || die "propagate failed"
build BASE; score BASE CONTROL
BASE_RES=$(grep -m1 PCC2W_RESULT "$OUT/BASE.out" | cut -d= -f2)
[ "$BASE_RES" = "60/60" ] || say "*** BASE IS NOT FULL ($BASE_RES): $(grep -m1 PCC2W_RED "$OUT/BASE.out") -- CONTINUING; read every cell with those rows SUBTRACTED ***"

mut() {  # $1 name  $2 expectation  $3 python edit body (operates on s)
    echo "$F  mutant $1" > "$T/.MUTANT"; cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"
    python3 - "$T/$F" "$3" <<'PYEOF'
import io, sys
p, body = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()
ns = {'s': s}
exec(body, ns)
io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(ns['s'])
PYEOF
    [ $? -eq 0 ] || die "$1: mutator failed"
    cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" && die "$1: file UNCHANGED after mutation"
    build "$1"; score "$1" "$2"
}
G1='\t\tif (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_GENCTL_LEN, "PCCTWO_GENCTL", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t}\n'
V1='\t\tif (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {\n\t\t\tpcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_VECBASE_LEN, "PCCTWO_VECBASE", len);\n\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t}\n'

mut M_DELETE  'rb genctl 0xbb' "a='$G1'; assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,'')"
mut M_NOBREAK 'rb genctl 0xbb + dcl 1' "a='$G1'; b=a.replace('\t\t\tbreak;\n',''); assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,b)"
mut M_ASSIGN  'latch same 2' "a='\t\td->warned |= (1u << bit);\n'; assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,'')"
mut M_SHARED  'latch two 1' "import re; n=len(re.findall(r'pcc2_width_decline\(d, PCC2_W_[A-Z]+_LEN,', s)); assert n==12, n; s=re.sub(r'pcc2_width_decline\(d, PCC2_W_[A-Z]+_LEN,', 'pcc2_width_decline(d, PCC2_W_GENCTL_LEN,', s)"
mut M_RDKILL  'hrd genctl exit1' "a='$G1'; b=a.replace('if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {', 'if (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_READ) { exit(1); }\n\t\tif (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {'); assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,b)"
mut M_OPKILL  'op vecbase exit1' "a='$V1'; b='\t\tif (len != 1) { exit(1); }\n'; assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,b)"
mut M_COMPOSE 'rb genctl 0xaa, rb vecbase 0xb0' "a='$G1'; b='\t\tif (len != 1 && writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {\n\t\t\tmemcpy(d->pcctwo_reg + relative_addr, data, len);\n\t\t\td->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_VECBASE] &= 0xf0;\n\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t}\n'; assert s.count(a)==1; s=s.replace(a,b)"

say "RESTORE from SEC + REBUILD + verify, THEN clear the sentinel"
cp "$SEC/$F" "$T/$F"; cmp -s "$T/$F" "$SEC/$F" || die "restore cmp FAILED -- sentinel KEPT"
build restore; rm -f "$T/.MUTANT"
say "  restored + REBUILT + verified, sentinel cleared"
say "KILLPCC2_DONE"

|---|---|---|---|---|

| M3 | `len != 1` -> `len > 2`, all twelve | - | 49/60 | KILLED (11 red) |

M4 is a RESULT, not a hole: `src/include/memory.h` defines MEM_READ 0 / MEM_WRITE 1,
`memory_rw.c` documents the writeflag as exactly those two, and MEM_DOWNGRADE (128) is
used only in `cpu_dyntrans.c` for a different argument.  The two forms cannot differ.

THE HOLE, in one line: the probe reaches **twelve register LABELS across only TEN
BLOCKS** (T1CTL/T2CTL share one latch bit, T1ICR/T2ICR share another).  `PCC2_W_IPL_LEN`
and `PCC2_W_MASK_LEN` have **zero rows** -- gate 14 names all 60 and none touches
0x3e or 0x3f.  And `latch two` is the only session that writes two blocks, so it pins
ONE of the 66 possible bit collisions.

## CONFIRMED AS OF TABLE 2 / M8

M6 = 60/60 (MASK announces then falls through) -- THIRD clean sweep.
M8 = 60/60 (guard DELETED at SCCTX) -- FOURTH, and the worst: SCCTX (0x1e) is one of
the FIVE GUEST-REACHABLE arms the contract claims to cover.  Its only wide-write rows
are `hwr scctx' (exit status) and `hwr scctx lv' (liveness) -- survival-only.  Its arm
masks `& 0x1f', so a declined write and an accepted one store DIFFERENT values, and no
row reads the value back.

The round's own M_DELETE deleted ALL TWELVE guards and died on `rb genctl'.  Deleting
ONE dies nowhere.  The detector catches the global edit and not the local one -- the
"three cells of a two-by-two" shape the carrier names.

## THE ROOT CAUSE, in one sentence

The reachability census in the product comment MIXES UNITS: "the five EVEN-offset
BLOCKS (GENCTL, TxCTL, TxICR, SCCERR, SCCTX)" + "the seven odd ones" = twelve, which
READS as all twelve blocks but is five BLOCKS plus seven LABELS.  T1CTL/T1ICR are
labels of blocks already counted in the five, and PCCTWO_IPL (0x3e) and PCCTWO_MASK
(0x3f) appear in NEITHER list.  The probe was built from that census, which is exactly
why it has those twelve labels and no IPL/MASK rows.

## Q4 -- what would it take to know

The only consumer of `dev_pcc2.c` is MACHINE_MVME88K_187 (one `device_add`, in
`machine_mvme88k.c`).  `machine_luna88k.c` contains ZERO references to pcc2, and the
project's m88k boot rig is **luna88k** -- so **no mvme187 OS boot exists here at all**.

Consequence: the round's implicit warrant "no measured driver wide-writes these
registers" has NO measurement behind it in this project, because there is no execution
path on which such a write could have been observed.  That does not make DECLINE wrong
-- it makes it MORE clearly right, since COMPOSE would invent per-lane semantics that
nothing available here can validate.

The decline latch is, however, exactly the right instrument for the question: a real
mvme187 boot would print `-- declined.  (once) ]` the first time a driver wide-writes
any of the twelve.  So `pcc2widecompose` should record that its answer is one boot
away IF an image is ever obtained, and is otherwise a driver-SOURCE question
(OpenBSD `sys/arch/mvme88k/dev/pcctwo.c` and friends -- grep bus_space_write_2/_4
against the PCCTWO offsets).  No BSD driver source is on disk.

M9 = 60/60 (guard DELETED at MASK) -- FIFTH clean sweep.

So both 60/60 scores are DETECTOR HOLES, not equivalences.

## WHY THE ROUND'S OWN TABLE DID NOT SEE THIS -- and it is visible in its own numbers

    M_OPKILL   58/60   red: op vecbase, op vecbase dcl        (kill reinstated at VECBASE)

Every mutant in the round's table is either GLOBAL (all twelve arms) or aimed at GENCTL

escapes completely (M1, 60/60).

for.  Pass 3c is that measurement, and it is the last floor question outstanding
before the commit.

The three shapes the round DID measure (delete / len>2 / kill) all make the arm STOP
ANNOUNCING, so they move the `dcl' row and `latch all' as well.  None of them exercises
the announce-then-land path these four rows exist to catch.  A row added for a shape
that no mutant in the table produces is exactly the vacuity the floor's clause (b)/(d)
machinery exists to detect -- which is why it has to be run, not asserted.

  M_FALL_SCCICR     MEASURED: rb sccicr got 0x18, want 0x1f -> FAIL, and it is the
                    SOLE red.  latch all 12 (green), op sccicr dcl 1 (green),
                    rb sccrx / rb scsiicr / op pscale noig all green.
                    0x18 = 0xb8 & 0x1f -- the neighbour's lane in an interrupt-control
                    register, exactly the value predicted at 14:10.

  The kill is PRECISE: only the mutated arm's own row moves.  That is the correct
  signature -- the shape leaves every other channel untouched by construction, so a
  one-row kill is the strongest result available, not a weak one.
  M_FALL_SCCRX      81/82  RED rb sccrx ALONE  (0xb8 & 0xdf = 0x98)

## VALUE-LEVEL CONFIRMATION, not just red/green

Each verified row caught the EXACT byte predicted at 14:10 from the arm's own mask,
which is stronger than a colour change -- it shows the row observes the real defect
rather than merely differing:

    rb sccicr   got 0x18   = 0xb8 & 0x1f   (SCCICR's mask)
    rb sccrx    got 0x98   = 0xb8 & 0xdf   (SCCRX's mask)

0xb8 is the LOW lane of the wide 0xaab8 -- i.e. the byte belonging to the NEXT address,
which is the whole lane hazard this round exists to prevent, reappearing at an arm the
78-row detector could not see.
  M_FALL_SCSIICR    81/82  RED rb scsiicr ALONE  (0xb8 & 0xdf = 0x98)
  M_FALL_PSCALEADJ  81/82  RED `op pscale noig' ALONE  got 1, want 0

## PASS 3c COMPLETE -- THE 82-ROW REPAIR CLOSES THE CLASS

  BASE3             82/82
  M_FALL_SCCICR     81/82  RED rb sccicr      0x18 (= 0xb8 & 0x1f)
  M_FALL_SCCRX      81/82  RED rb sccrx       0x98 (= 0xb8 & 0xdf)
  M_FALL_SCSIICR    81/82  RED rb scsiicr     0x98 (= 0xb8 & 0xdf)
  M_FALL_PSCALEADJ  81/82  RED op pscale noig 1    (the print a decline never reaches)
  RESTORE PART 1 OK; SENTINEL CLEARED; clone back to 4c0cfc6d (b78bb0f blob).

All four predicted at 14:10 before any cell ran, INCLUDING which single row would move
and, for the three storing arms, the exact byte.  Four for four.

## THE SHAPE OF THE RESULT

Each arm is killed by the channel MATCHED TO ITS OWN BODY, not by a copied row:
  three arms STORE  -> a readback catches the neighbour lane
  PSCALEADJ PRINTS  -> the message it should never reach, counted at zero
That is why the count-based rows cannot help: under announce-then-land `latch all'
stays 12 and every `dcl' row stays 1 BY CONSTRUCTION.  The repair understood this and
built per-arm channels rather than one more counter.

## THE CHAIN, END TO END

  60 rows   six clean sweeps (smallest ONE CHARACTER)          -> DO NOT CLOSE
  78 rows   kills all six; but four arms had NO channel        -> 4 x 78/78 escape
  82 rows   per-arm channels                                   -> 4 x 81/82, all killed

 succeeded in 166ms:
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: """pcc2widthkills DETECTOR -- the twelve width-guard blocks in DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2).
   3: 
   4: WHAT THE FIX DID.  Twelve `if (len != 1) { fatal(...); exit(1); }' blocks (ten kills and
   5: two #119 function-static latches) ended the host on a guest-chosen access width.  The
   6: tree's own header says PCC2 registers "can be accessed as bytes, two-bytes, or
   7: four-bytes", so the width is LEGAL.  The fix is a DIRECTION SPLIT:
   8: 
   9:     READS   ACCEPT -- the memcpy above the switch already served them, raw bytes in
  10:             address order, width-correct by construction.  The guard discarded a
  11:             correct answer.
  12:     WRITES  DECLINE + per-instance latched announce -- every write arm uses `idata',
  13:             the big-endian-composed value, which is "the addressed byte" ONLY at
  14:             len==1.  Deleting the guard would store the NEIGHBOUR's lane.
  15: 
  16: CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this detector is designed to catch:
  17: 
  18:   CATCHES
  19:     * reinstatement of the kill at any of the SIX GUEST-reachable blocks (survival
  20:       rows paired with a liveness observable, because exit status cannot tell
  21:       "declined and quit" from "died with status 0");
  22:     * *** pass 2: the two FOLDED-IN arms, IPL and MASK, had NO ROW -- twelve blocks
  23:       converted, ten given rows, and the shipped contract did not say so.  A seat
  24:       found it by sibling parity: MASK is GENCTL's twin and had none of GENCTL's rows.
  25:       Repaired: IPL is EVEN and therefore the SIXTH guest-reachable block (survival +
  26:       message rows); MASK is odd (operator rows) and gets GENCTL's readback. ***
  27:     * reinstatement at any of the seven OPERATOR-reachable blocks, through the
  28:       debugger's `put h' at an odd address -- MEASURED to warn and store and kill
  29:       (artifacts/pcc2widthkills/operator_reach_20260903.out);
  30:     * *** DELETE instead of decline on the write side -- the lane hazard.  `st.h
  31:       0xAABB' to GENCTL must leave GENCTL unchanged; a bare deletion stores 0xBB,
  32:       VECBASE's lane, because (uint8_t) idata is the HIGHEST-address byte ***;
  33:     * an accidental per-lane compose (GENCTL would read 0xAA);
  34:     * the value a wide READ composes -- lane order and width -- not merely survival;
  35:     * loss of the latch in either direction: dropped `|=' floods (same site twice
  36:       must announce ONCE), and one bit shared across blocks silences (two sites must
  37:       announce TWICE);
  38:     * the de-escalation clause: byte writes at the LEGAL width at every reachable
  39:       arm still land, read back through each arm's own mask, with NO decline message.
  40: 
  41:   DOES NOT CATCH, stated so an escape is outside the contract rather than an argument
  42:     * per-instance vs process-global latch state: mvme187 instantiates ONE pcc2, so no
  43:       session can hold two instances.  The bitfield is per-instance by construction;
  44:       nothing here measures it;
  45:     * pcc2c040kill -- a LEGAL byte write of 0x04 to GENCTL still ends the host in
  46:       reassert_interrupts().  Every GENCTL lane value here keeps bit 0x04 CLEAR so no
  47:       row dies for that reason;
